During this flagship project, the combination of chronic sleep disruption, performance pressure, and constant decision‑making seemed to intensify those swings: after an especially driven period where she over‑committed to process‑improvement initiatives and after‑work activities, she hit a three‑week stretch in which her usual analytical clarity collapsed, she began missing key details in SAP reports, and she spent hours second‑guessing basic choices, alarming her team and supervisor. maybe three or four hours a night—staying up doing slide decks and messaging colleagues in different time zones—and instead of feeling tired I woke up convinced I was totally fine and didn’t really need more sleep.\n- S037 (Pressured_Speech): My coworkers joked that I was on “1.5x speed” because in meetings I’d jump in constantly, talking fast and jumping from idea to idea without pausing long enough for anyone to respond, and I’d feel almost physically uncomfortable if the room went quiet.\n- S040 (Increased_Goal_Activity): I suddenly packed my calendar with extra plant visits, after‑work networking drinks, and side projects like redesigning our reporting dashboards, chasing all of it at once with this restless drive that made it impossible to just go home and switch off.\n▸ Additional When a routine performance check‑in turned into a candid discussion about her recent decline in concentration and inability to keep up with previously manageable tasks, her HR partner suggested an occupational medicine consult, which led to a referral for psychiatric evaluation.\n- Chief Complaint: “Lately I can’t think straight at work—decisions that used to be easy now leave me stuck and second‑guessing everything.”\n▸ Patient-specific Symptom Manifestations\n- S024 (Depressed_Mood): For the past few weeks it’s felt like this heavy, gray film has settled over everything, and I catch myself staring at my spreadsheets or out the hotel window feeling utterly hollow and on the verge of tears for no clear reason.\n- S026 (Weight_Appetite_Change): I used to be the one suggesting new places for team dinners, but lately even when I’m starving after a 12-hour day I just pick at a few bites and push the rest around, and my clothes are starting to hang looser because I just have no interest in eating.\n- S027 (Sleep_Disturbance): Most nights I lie in bed exhausted but wired, taking an hour or more to fall asleep and then waking up at 3 or 4 a.m. with work worries looping in my head, and even on the rare night I get a full stretch it still feels like I haven’t rested at all.\n- S028 (Psychomotor_Changes): My team has commented that I move like I’m underwater now—walking slowly between offices, just sitting and staring at the screen, and even reaching for my notebook or typing an email feels like it takes deliberate, physical effort.\n- S031 (Diminished_Thinking): At work my mind feels wrapped in fog: I reread the same simple SAP report lines over and over, can’t hold numbers in my head, and I end up agonizing for half an hour over whether to approve a routine purchase order that used to be a two‑minute decision.\n- S033 (Elevated_Irritable_Mood): A couple of weeks before this crash I had this stretch where I was either buzzing with excitement about the project or snapping at people over tiny delays, flipping from feeling like everything was brilliant to being irrationally irritated within the same meeting.\n- S034 (Increased_Activity): During that high period I was bouncing around the plants nonstop—volunteering for extra kaizen events, reorganizing my entire workflow, booking back‑to‑back meetings, and pacing my hotel room at midnight planning new initiatives because I just couldn’t sit still.\n- S035 (Inflated_Self_Esteem): When I was on that upswing, I honestly caught myself thinking, ‘No one else here really gets lean like I do, I can probably turn this whole division around if they just let me run with my ideas,’ which is way more grandiose than how I usually see myself.\n- S036 (Decreased_Sleep): In that same phase I was sleeping maybe three or four hours a night—staying up doing slide decks and messaging colleagues in different time zones—and instead of feeling tired I woke up convinced I was totally fine and didn’t really need more sleep.\n- S037 (Pressured_Speech): My coworkers joked that I was on “1.5x speed” because in meetings I’d jump in constantly, talking fast and jumping from idea to idea without pausing long enough for anyone to respond, and I’d feel almost physically uncomfortable if the room went quiet.\n- S040 (Increased_Goal_Activity): I suddenly packed my calendar with extra plant visits, after‑work networking drinks, and side projects like redesigning our reporting dashboards, chasing all of it at once with this restless drive that made it impossible to just go home and switch off.\n▸ Additional Diagnostic Requirements\n- functional_impairment: Over these past few months my work has really suffered—I’ve missed deadlines, had to hand off parts of the flagship project to colleagues, and my manager has hinted that if I can’t get back to my usual level, they may need to reconsider my role on the transformation team.\n- duration: Looking back, I’ve been cycling through these kinds of highs and crashes since around my mid‑twenties, but it’s only in the last year on this big project that the pattern has become impossible for me and my coworkers to ignore.\n- depressive_episode_duration: The severe issues with my concentration really escalated after the stress of my promotion to lead a high-pressure lean transformation project, and this current low spell has been dragging on for about three months now.\n\nDoctor: During those 'high' periods you mentioned experiencing since your mid-twenties, did you ever have times where you felt unusually energetic, needed very little sleep, or felt like your thoughts were racing and you were taking on too many projects at once?\n\nPatient: Yes, absolutely. During those times I'd sleep only three or four hours a night but feel completely fine, and I'd pack my schedule with extra projects and plant visits. My coworkers even joked that I was on \"1.5x speed\" because I talked so fast and jumped constantly from one idea to the next.\n\nDoctor: During your current three-month low, have you experienced any thoughts of wanting to end your life, or have you felt completely unable to function in your daily activities?\n\nPatient: I haven't had any thoughts of wanting to end my life, but my ability to function at work has been severely impacted. I've been missing key deadlines and had to hand off parts of my flagship project to colleagues. My manager has even warned me that they may have to reconsider my role on the transformation team if I can't get back to my usual level.\n\nDoctor: During this current three-month period, have you felt completely unable to function in your daily activities, such as being unable to eat, bathe, or take care of your basic needs, or is your impairment primarily limited to your work performance?\n\nPatient: While I'm still managing basic hygiene, the impairment is definitely most severe at work where I'm missing deadlines and facing a potential change in my role. However, my daily life is absolutely affected too; I've completely lost my appetite and barely eat, and even basic movements feel like I'm trying to walk underwater. Everything just takes immense physical effort right now.\n\nDoctor: Beyond the fatigue and appetite loss, have you experienced any periods during these past three months where you felt completely unable to experience any pleasure or interest in your usual hobbies, or felt a distinct, heavy sense of worthlessness or guilt?"

I haven't really experienced those specific feelings of guilt or a loss of interest in my hobbies. Mostly, I just feel this heavy, hollow emptiness and a complete lack of energy.

I've been missing key deadlines and had to hand off parts of my flagship project to colleagues. My manager has even warned me that they may have to reconsider my role on the transformation team if I can't get back to my usual level.\n\nDoctor: During this current three-month period, have you felt completely unable to function in your daily activities, such as being unable to eat, bathe, or take care of your basic needs, or is your impairment primarily limited to your work performance?\n\nPatient: While I'm still managing basic hygiene, the impairment is definitely most severe at work where I'm missing deadlines and facing a potential change in my role. However, my daily life is absolutely affected too; I've completely lost my appetite and barely eat, and even basic movements feel like I'm trying to walk underwater. Everything just takes immense physical effort right now.\n\nDoctor: Beyond the fatigue and appetite loss, have you experienced any periods during these past three months where you felt completely unable to experience any pleasure or interest in your usual hobbies, or felt a distinct, heavy sense of worthlessness or guilt?\n\nPatient: I haven't really experienced those specific feelings of guilt or a loss of interest in my hobbies. Mostly, I just feel this heavy, hollow emptiness and a complete lack of energy.\n\nDoctor: During these past three months, have you felt so slowed down physically and mentally that others have noticed you moving or speaking much more slowly than usual?\n\nPatient: Yes, during this three-month low, I have felt incredibly slowed down both physically and mentally. Subtypes: Childlike Silliness, Stupor, Catalepsy, Waxy Flexibility, Mutism, Negativism, Posturing, Mannerism, Stereotypy, Agitation, Grimacing, Echolalia, Echopraxia\n[S023] Negative_Symptoms: A state where emotions, motivation, speech, or social interest that should normally be present are diminished or lost.  Subtypes: Diminished Emotional Expression, Avolition, Alogia, Anhedonia, Asociality\n[S024] Depressed_Mood: A state where a sad, empty, or hopeless mood persists.  Subtypes: Depressed Mood, Tearfulness, Sadness, Emptiness, Emotional Numbness\n[S025] Loss_of_Interest: A state where interest or pleasure in previously enjoyed activities is markedly diminished.  Subtypes: Loss of Interest, Social Withdrawal, Loss of Motivation, Anhedonia, Indifference to Achievement\n[S026] Weight_Appetite_Change: Significant increase or decrease in appetite or weight.  Subtypes: Significant Weight Loss, Weight Gain, Decrease in Appetite, Increase in Appetite\n[S027] Sleep_Disturbance: A state where there is a significant change in the quantity or quality of sleep.  Subtypes: Initial Insomnia, Middle Insomnia, Early Morning Awakening, Hypersomnia, Nightmare Disorder, Non-Restorative Sleep\n[S028] Psychomotor_Changes: Observable increase or decrease in psychomotor activity.  Subtypes: Psychomotor Agitation, Psychomotor Retardation (Motor), Psychomotor Retardation (Speech)\n[S029] Fatigue: Persistent fatigue that is not relieved by rest and a marked lack of energy.  Subtypes: Chronic Fatigue, Physical Fatigue, Mental Fatigue, Loss of Energy\n[S030] Worthlessness_Guilt: Feelings of worthlessness or excessive or inappropriate guilt (which may be delusional; not merely self-reproach or guilt about being sick).  Subtypes: Feelings of Worthlessness, Ruminative Guilt, Excessive or Inappropriate Guilt, Delusional Guilt\n[S031] Diminished_Thinking: Diminished ability to think or concentrate, or indecisiveness.  Subtypes: Reduced Concentration, Indecisiveness, Subjective Memory Complaints, Clouded Thinking\n[S032] Death_Suicide: Recurrent thoughts of death, recurrent suicidal ideation, or a suicide attempt.  Subtypes: Recurrent Thoughts of Death, Suicidal Ideation, Suicidal Impulse, Specific Plan for Suicide, Suicide Attempt\n[S083] Hopelessness: A feeling of despair or having no hope for the future. Subtypes: Elevated Mood, Irritable Mood, Mood Lability\n[S034] Increased_Activity: Abnormally and persistently increased goal-directed activity or energy.  Subtypes: Increased Energy, Hyperactivity, Psychomotor Agitation\n[S035] Inflated_Self_Esteem: Inflated self-esteem or grandiosity (may range from uncritical self-confidence to delusional proportions). Subtypes: Reduced Sleep Duration, Loss of Sleep Need, Indifference to Insomnia\n[S037] Pressured_Speech: More talkative than usual or pressure to keep talking.  Subtypes: Talkativeness, Rapid Speech, Pressured Speech\n[S038] Racing_Thoughts: Flight of ideas or subjective experience that thoughts are racing.  Subtypes: Racing Thoughts, Flooding of Thoughts, Inability to Control Thoughts\n[S039] Manic_Distractibility: Attention is too easily drawn to unimportant or irrelevant external stimuli. Subtypes: Occupational Hyperactivity, Social Hyperactivity, Hypersexuality, Psychomotor Agitation\n[S041] Painful_Consequence_Behavior: Excessive involvement in activities that have a high potential for painful consequences. Subtypes: Eating Very Small Amounts, Skipping Meals, Exclusion of Specific Food Groups, Strict Calorie Restriction\n[S044] Low_Weight: A body weight that is significantly below the minimum level of health for age, sex, developmental trajectory, and physical health.  Subtypes: Continuous Weight Loss, Insistence on Low Weight\n[S045] Fear_of_Weight_Gain: Intense fear of gaining weight or of becoming fat, even though at a significantly low weight.  Subtypes: Fear of Weight Gain, Fear of Specific Foods, Obsession with Weight Gain\n[S046] Body_Image_Weight_Shape_Disturbance: Undue influence of body weight or shape on self-evaluation.  Subtypes: Distorted Body Image, Self-Worth Dependent on Weight\n[S047] Compensatory_Behavior_NonPurging: Recurrent inappropriate compensatory behaviors to prevent weight gain without purging (direct removal of food).  Subtypes: Fasting, Excessive Exercise\n[S048] Compensatory_Behavior_Purging: Recurrent inappropriate compensatory behaviors involving the expulsion of food or fluids to prevent weight gain.  Subtypes: Self-Induced Vomiting, Misuse of Medications\n[S049] Eating_Large_Amount: Eating, in a discrete period of time, an amount of food that is definitely larger than what most individuals would eat in a similar period of time under similar circumstances.\n[S050] Loss_of_Control: A sense of lack of control over eating during the episode (e.g., a feeling that one cannot stop eating or control what or how much one is eating).\n[S051] Rapid_Eating: Eating much more rapidly than normal.\n[S052] Eating_Until_Uncomfortably_Full: Eating until feeling uncomfortably full.\n[S053] Eating_Without_Hunger: Eating large amounts of food when not feeling physically hungry.\n[S054] Eating_Alone_Due_to_Embarrassment: Eating alone because of feeling embarrassed by how much one is eating.\n[S055] Negative_Affect_After_Binge: Feeling disgusted with oneself, depressed, or very guilty afterward.\n[S084] Low_Weight_Severity_Non_Recognition: Persistent lack of recognition of the seriousness of the current low body weight. Subtypes: Failure to Control, Intrusive Worry, Chaining of Worry\n[S058] Restlessness_Tension: Restlessness, or feeling keyed up or on edge. Subtypes: Interference by Worry, Inability to Sustain Attention, Mind Going Blank\n[S060] Irritability: A state of reacting sensitively or sharply to minor stimuli.  Subtypes: Low Frustration Tolerance, Snappiness, Exhausted Patience\n[S061] Muscle_Tension: A state where muscles are continuously tense, stiff, and accompanied by discomfort.  Subtypes: Neck and Shoulder Tension, Jaw Tension, Generalized Muscle Aches, Chronic Stiffness\n[S062] Specific_Phobic_Fear: Marked fear or anxiety about a specific object or situation that is out of proportion to the actual danger.
